    *   Clear problem statement and well-defined approach.
    *   Novel combination of techniques: Modality bridging for automated CoT generation and PTST for RL fine-tuning.
    *   Strong empirical results, demonstrating performance gains.
    *   The paper is well written and the results are presented effectively.
*   **Weaknesses:**
    *   The reliance on existing models (Qwen and DeepSeek-R1) for data generation raises questions about potential biases and limitations inherited from those models.
    *   The "human-like" reasoning claim, while supported by examples, requires more rigorous evaluation.
    *   The paper could benefit from a more detailed analysis of the generated CoT data and the impact of PTST at different training stages. Showing more in-depth quantitative evaluation of the CoTs' quality and diversity would strengthen the study.
    *   The generalizability of the approach to other multimodal tasks and datasets should be further investigated.

*   **Potential Influence:**  The Vision-R1 approach has the potential to influence future research on training MLLMs for complex reasoning tasks. The techniques for automated CoT generation and progressive training could be adopted and adapted by other researchers.  The clear demonstration of the effectiveness of this method could encourage more research focused on RL fine-tuning with structured learning approaches.

**Score:** 7.5

**Rationale:** The paper presents a novel and well-executed approach to incentivizing reasoning in MLLMs. The combination of modality bridging for automated CoT data generation and PTST for RL fine-tuning is innovative and demonstrates significant performance gains. While the reliance on existing models and the need for a more in-depth analysis of the generated data represent weaknesses, the paper's potential to enable complex reasoning in smaller MLLMs justifies a strong score. The paper provides valuable insights and motivates further research in this area.

- **Classification**: cs.CV
- **Score**: 7/10

### Effectiveness of Zero-shot-CoT in Japanese Prompts
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2503.06765v1)
- **Authors**: Shusuke Takayama, Ian Frank
- **Abstract**: We compare the effectiveness of zero-shot Chain-of-Thought (CoT) prompting in Japanese and English using ChatGPT-3.5 and 4o-mini. The technique of zero-shot CoT, which involves appending a phrase such as "Let's think step by step" to a prompt to encourage reasoning before answering, has been shown to offer LLM performance improvements in mathematical and reasoning tasks, particularly in English. We investigate how these effects transfer to Japanese using the Japanese Multi-task Language Understanding Benchmark (JMMLU) and the Multi-task Language Understanding Benchmark (MMLU). Our results show that while zero-shot CoT prompting can lead to notable performance gains for some prompt categories in GPT-3.5, its impact in GPT-4o-mini is associated with significant performance declines. However, for Japanese prompts there remain certain categories, such as college mathematics and abstract algebra, that still exhibit improvements, despite the broader trend of diminishing effectiveness in more advanced models.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper "Effectiveness of Zero-Shot-CoT in Japanese Prompts":

**Summary:**

The paper investigates the effectiveness of zero-shot Chain-of-Thought (CoT) prompting in both Japanese and English using ChatGPT-3.5 and GPT-4o-mini. It compares model performance on MMLU and JMMLU benchmarks with and without CoT. The key finding is that while CoT sometimes improves performance on specific task categories, especially for GPT-3.5, it generally leads to a decline in overall performance, particularly for GPT-4o-mini. The study explores differences in how CoT affects the models in Japanese versus English and proposes possible explanations related to internal reasoning capabilities and clause complexity.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The paper's primary contribution lies in its cross-lingual analysis of zero-shot CoT, specifically focusing on Japanese. While CoT has been extensively studied in English, exploring its effectiveness in other languages is a valuable step. The comparison between GPT-3.5 and GPT-4o-mini is also important, as it highlights how the technique's utility evolves with model advancements. However, the core methodology is relatively standard – applying a known technique and measuring performance, which limits the novelty.
*   **Significance:** The findings have implications for prompt engineering in multilingual contexts. The observation that CoT can be detrimental in advanced models, and that this effect differs across languages, raises questions about how best to leverage prompt engineering techniques as LLMs evolve. The paper also touches on the nuances of cultural or linguistic dependencies of prompt effectiveness. However, the practical significance is tempered by the fact that the improvements/declines are relatively small in many cases, especially with modern, instruction-tuned models.
*   **Strengths:**
    *   **Cross-lingual Analysis:**  The core strength is the systematic comparison of CoT effectiveness between English and Japanese.
    *   **Model Comparison:** Examining both GPT-3.5 and GPT-4o-mini provides insights into the technique's performance across different model architectures.
    *   **Dataset Variety:** The use of both MMLU and JMMLU allows for a more robust assessment of the technique's generalizability.
    *   **Clear Presentation:**  The paper clearly presents its results and provides insightful interpretations.

*   **Weaknesses:**
    *   **Limited Scope:** The study focuses exclusively on zero-shot CoT. Exploring other prompting strategies might offer a more comprehensive understanding of prompt engineering in Japanese.
    *   **Shallow Explanations:** The explanations for the observed differences are somewhat speculative. While internal reasoning capabilities and clause complexity are plausible factors, further investigation is needed to confirm their influence.
    *   **Lack of Statistical Rigor**: While the paper presents some statistical test results, there is a lack of justification or explanation for why these tests were chosen. Also, no p-value correction is applied, which is crucial when running multiple statistical tests.

*   **Potential Influence:** The paper can contribute to a more nuanced understanding of prompt engineering for LLMs. It emphasizes the importance of considering language-specific effects and the evolution of model architectures when selecting prompting strategies. It may encourage further research into how to adapt prompt engineering techniques for multilingual tasks and how to optimize prompting for newer, more advanced LLMs.

**Overall:**

The paper provides valuable insights into the cross-lingual aspects of zero-shot CoT prompting, highlighting potential pitfalls and the need for adaptive strategies. While its methodology is not groundbreaking and the explanations are somewhat speculative, the empirical findings are relevant to the prompt engineering community. The study identifies an interesting trend and may stimulate future research in this area.

Score: 5

Rationale:

The paper's strength lies in its empirical examination of CoT in Japanese. However, the methodology is not particularly innovative, and the explanations for the results are speculative. The lack of rigorous statistical analysis weakens the conclusions. The paper's overall significance is moderate; it offers useful information but doesn't fundamentally change our understanding of prompt engineering. Therefore, a score of 5 reflects a contribution that is worthwhile but not transformative.

- **Classification**: cs.CL
- **Score**: 5/10

### Large Language Models Are Effective Human Annotation Assistants, But Not Good Independent Annotators
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2503.06778v1)
- **Authors**: Feng Gu, Zongxia Li, Carlos Rafael Colon, Benjamin Evans, Ishani Mondal, Jordan Lee Boyd-Graber
- **Abstract**: Event annotation is important for identifying market changes, monitoring breaking news, and understanding sociological trends. Although expert annotators set the gold standards, human coding is expensive and inefficient. Unlike information extraction experiments that focus on single contexts, we evaluate a holistic workflow that removes irrelevant documents, merges documents about the same event, and annotates the events. Although LLM-based automated annotations are better than traditional TF-IDF-based methods or Event Set Curation, they are still not reliable annotators compared to human experts. However, adding LLMs to assist experts for Event Set Curation can reduce the time and mental effort required for Variable Annotation. When using LLMs to extract event variables to assist expert annotators, they agree more with the extracted variables than fully automated LLMs for annotation.
- **Summary**: Here is a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper investigates the effectiveness of Large Language Models (LLMs) as assistants in event annotation, a task crucial for various applications like monitoring news and understanding sociological trends. Unlike standard information extraction scenarios that focus on individual contexts, this study evaluates a holistic workflow encompassing document filtering, event set curation (grouping documents about the same event), and event annotation. The study compares LLM-based automation with traditional methods and human expert performance. While LLMs outperform methods like TF-IDF in event set curation, they don't match the reliability of human annotators. However, they can significantly aid human annotators by extracting event variables, reducing both time and effort in the variable annotation phase.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The paper's novelty lies in its focus on a complete event annotation workflow, combining event set creation *and* variable annotation, rather than examining a narrower task. While LLM-assisted annotation is not entirely new, applying it to the specific challenges of event annotation in a real-world setting (using the GTD database) and systematically evaluating its impact on human efficiency is a notable contribution. The decomposition of the annotation process into multiple stages and the identification of where LLMs can be most effective is insightful. Comparing human-AI collaboration with fully automated LLM solutions adds to the novelty.

*   **Significance:** The findings have practical significance for organizations like GTD that rely on event annotation. By demonstrating that LLMs can reduce annotation time and improve human-AI agreement, the paper offers a path toward more efficient and scalable data collection. The identification of specific areas where LLMs excel (e.g., variable extraction assistance) and where they fall short (e.g., independent annotation) is valuable for guiding the deployment of AI in this domain. The analysis of error types, specifically related to underspecified instructions, is also a significant practical contribution for improving prompts and guidelines. However, the focus is primarily on a single domain (terrorism event annotation) with possibly biased model, which might limit generalizability.

*   **Strengths:**
    *   Holistic Evaluation: Comprehensive evaluation of the complete event annotation pipeline, rather than isolated tasks.
    *   Real-World Application: Applies the methods to a realistic dataset (GTD) and a practical workflow.
    *   Error Analysis: Thorough analysis of error sources and limitations of LLMs.
    *   Detailed Comparison: Compares LLM performance with human annotators and traditional methods (TF-IDF, etc.).
    *   Quantified Impact: Quantifies the reduction in annotation time and the increase in agreement.

*   **Weaknesses:**
    *   Limited Generalizability: The study is focused on a specific domain (terrorism events) and its specific bias.
    *   Model Dependence: The performance is likely tied to the specific LLM used (GPT-4o-mini), and further work is needed to evaluate other models.
    *   Lack of Deeper Error Discussion: Discussion is limited and could be expanded.
    *   Lack of Ablation Studies: Not fully discussing impact of event curation in annotation efficiency.

*   **Potential Influence:** The paper is likely to influence future research on human-AI collaboration for event annotation, especially in security and social science domains. It provides a roadmap for integrating LLMs into existing workflows and highlights the importance of carefully considering the strengths and weaknesses of AI models in different stages of the annotation process.

**Score: 7**

**Rationale:**

The paper offers a solid contribution to the field by demonstrating the practical benefits and limitations of using LLMs as assistants in a complex event annotation workflow. While the novelty isn't groundbreaking, the systematic evaluation, practical focus, and actionable insights justify a score of 7. The limitations regarding domain specificity and model dependence prevent a higher score. The clear discussion on error analysis as well as the impact of automation on reducing manual coding time is well discussed, and helps justify the score above a 5.

- **Classification**: cs.CL
- **Score**: 7/10

### Infinite Leagues Under the Sea: Photorealistic 3D Underwater Terrain Generation by Latent Fractal Diffusion Models
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2503.06784v1)
- **Authors**: Tianyi Zhang, Weiming Zhi, Joshua Mangelson, Matthew Johnson-Roberson
- **Abstract**: This paper tackles the problem of generating representations of underwater 3D terrain. Off-the-shelf generative models, trained on Internet-scale data but not on specialized underwater images, exhibit downgraded realism, as images of the seafloor are relatively uncommon. To this end, we introduce DreamSea, a generative model to generate hyper-realistic underwater scenes. DreamSea is trained on real-world image databases collected from underwater robot surveys. Images from these surveys contain massive real seafloor observations and covering large areas, but are prone to noise and artifacts from the real world. We extract 3D geometry and semantics from the data with visual foundation models, and train a diffusion model that generates realistic seafloor images in RGBD channels, conditioned on novel fractal distribution-based latent embeddings. We then fuse the generated images into a 3D map, building a 3DGS model supervised by 2D diffusion priors which allows photorealistic novel view rendering. DreamSea is rigorously evaluated, demonstrating the ability to robustly generate large-scale underwater scenes that are consistent, diverse, and photorealistic. Our work drives impact in multiple domains, spanning filming, gaming, and robot simulation.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper introduces DreamSea, a novel generative model designed to create photorealistic 3D underwater terrain. It addresses the challenges of generating realistic underwater scenes due to the scarcity of specialized training data and the limitations of using generic pre-trained models. DreamSea leverages real-world underwater robot survey data, visual foundation models (VFMs) to extract 3D geometry and semantics, and a diffusion model conditioned on fractal distribution-based latent embeddings. This allows for controlled generation of diverse and consistent underwater scenes, which are then fused into a 3D Gaussian Splatting (3DGS) model for novel view rendering. The paper highlights the use of fractal processes to control the appearance of the generated terrain and presents results demonstrating the model's ability to produce large-scale, photorealistic underwater environments suitable for applications such as filming, gaming, and robot simulation.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The core novelty lies in the combination of several techniques applied specifically to the challenging domain of underwater scene generation. While individual components like diffusion models, VFMs, and 3DGS are not new, their integration within the DreamSea framework, the use of fractal latent embeddings for spatial control, and its application to sparse underwater data constitutes a unique contribution. The fractal embedding approach to generate spatially consistent underwater terrain is innovative.
*   **Significance:** Generating realistic underwater environments is vital for underwater robot simulation, marine conservation, and visualization. The capacity to generate such environments from minimal, unannotated real-world data has significant potential. DreamSea addresses a real-world problem with practical application areas.
*   **Strengths:**
    *   **Addresses a Challenging Problem:** The paper tackles a domain where data is limited and existing generative models struggle.
    *   **Integration of Techniques:** DreamSea skillfully combines VFMs, diffusion models, and 3DGS, demonstrating the power of combining existing methods.
    *   **Fractal Embedding Approach:** The novel use of fractal processes to control the spatial consistency and diversity of the generated terrain is a significant strength.
    *   **Qualitative Results:** The generated underwater scenes are visually appealing and demonstrate the model's ability to capture the diversity of underwater environments.
*   **Weaknesses:**
    *   **Relative Depth:** The model only estimates relative depth, requiring external sensors or methods for absolute scale recovery. This limitation hinders direct use in applications demanding absolute 3D measurements, particularly robotics and mapping.
    *   **Top-Down View Bias:** Training is limited to top-down views, restricting the diversity of viewing angles. This view bias should be addressed by mentioning as one of the limitation or future work.
    *   **Evaluation metrics could be improved** the CLIP/DINO MSE are good, but lack comparison to other methods to highlight the novelty. A user study could also validate the photorealistic capabilities of the trained model.
    *   **Limited Comparison in results**: results generated through DreamSea is not compared with results with other state of the art generation techniques, which leads to no way of knowing whether it generates better results than others or not.

*   **Potential Influence:** The work has the potential to influence the field of underwater robotics, simulation, and marine visualization. It provides a practical approach for generating realistic underwater environments from limited data, enabling better robot training, virtual exploration, and scientific visualization. The fractal embedding technique may be applicable to other spatial generative tasks.

**Rationale for the Score:**

The paper's novelty lies in the specific combination of existing techniques and the novel fractal embedding approach, rather than a revolutionary breakthrough in any single component. While the visual results are impressive, the limitations, particularly regarding relative depth, top-down view, and evaluation make it so the paper's impact on the field is likely to be notable but not transformative. It's a strong contribution, but there is room for improvement and extension.

**Score: 7**

- **Classification**: cs.GR
- **Score**: 7/10

### GenDR: Lightning Generative Detail Restorator
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2503.06790v1)
- **Authors**: Yan Wang, Shijie Zhao, Kai Chen, Kexin Zhang, Junlin Li, Li Zhang
- **Abstract**: Recent research applying text-to-image (T2I) diffusion models to real-world super-resolution (SR) has achieved remarkable success. However, fundamental misalignments between T2I and SR targets result in a dilemma between inference speed and detail fidelity. Specifically, T2I tasks prioritize multi-step inversion to synthesize coherent outputs aligned with textual prompts and shrink the latent space to reduce generating complexity. Contrariwise, SR tasks preserve most information from low-resolution input while solely restoring high-frequency details, thus necessitating sufficient latent space and fewer inference steps. To bridge the gap, we present a one-step diffusion model for generative detail restoration, GenDR, distilled from a tailored diffusion model with larger latent space. In detail, we train a new SD2.1-VAE16 (0.9B) via representation alignment to expand latent space without enlarging the model size. Regarding step-distillation, we propose consistent score identity distillation (CiD) that incorporates SR task-specific loss into score distillation to leverage more SR priors and align the training target. Furthermore, we extend CiD with adversarial learning and representation alignment (CiDA) to enhance perceptual quality and accelerate training. We also polish the pipeline to achieve a more efficient inference. Experimental results demonstrate that GenDR achieves state-of-the-art performance in both quantitative metrics and visual fidelity.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper "GenDR: Lightning Generative Detail Restorator" introduces a novel one-step diffusion model for real-world super-resolution (SR), specifically designed for generative detail restoration.  It addresses the perceived mismatch between text-to-image (T2I) diffusion models and SR tasks, arguing that SR requires a larger latent space and fewer inference steps than T2I models typically utilize.  GenDR achieves this through several key innovations: (1) a new SD2.1-VAE16 (0.9B) model trained with representation alignment to expand the latent space without increasing model size, (2) a Consistent score identity Distillation (CiD) method that incorporates SR task-specific loss into score distillation, and (3) the CiDA (CiD with Adversarial learning and Representation Alignment) which enhances perceptual quality and speeds up training.  The paper demonstrates state-of-the-art performance in both quantitative metrics and visual fidelity.

**Critical Evaluation:**

* **Novelty:** The paper presents several potentially novel aspects:

    *   **SD2.1-VAE16:** While based on existing architectures, the deliberate training of a 16-channel VAE tailored for SR tasks, specifically with representation alignment (REPA),  is potentially novel and addresses a specific limitation of standard 4-channel VAEs.
    *   **Consistent Score Identity Distillation (CiD) and CiDA:** This is the core technical contribution. Integrating SR-specific losses into the score distillation process, along with adversarial learning and representation alignment, appears to be a novel way to leverage diffusion models for SR. The addition of adversarial learning and representation alignment on top of score distillation (CiDA) to enhance perceptual quality is also a significant contribution.

* **Significance:**

    *   **Performance:** The paper claims and demonstrates (through metrics and visual comparisons) state-of-the-art performance. If verified by the community, this is significant.  The speed of inference (one-step) is also a major advantage.
    *   **Addressing a key challenge:** The explicit tackling of the discrepancy between T2I objectives and SR objectives is a valuable contribution.  Many previous SR works leveraging diffusion models have simply fine-tuned or adapted existing T2I architectures.  GenDR proposes modifications better suited for SR.
    *   **Practicality:** The emphasis on efficient inference (one-step model and simplified pipeline) makes the approach more attractive for real-world applications.

* **Strengths:**

    *   The paper provides a clear problem definition, identifying the specific challenges of applying T2I models to SR.
    *   The technical approach is well-motivated, with each component (SD2.1-VAE16, CiD, CiDA) designed to address a specific limitation.
    *   The experimental results are comprehensive, including quantitative metrics, visual comparisons, and ablation studies.
    *   The ablation studies are insightful, demonstrating the impact of each proposed component.
    *   The writing is generally clear.

* **Weaknesses:**

    *   **Reliance on Existing Techniques:** Although combined in a novel way, the individual components (SD2.1 based UNet, Representation Alignment, distillation techniques) are based on known ideas.  The *combination* and *tailoring* for SR are the primary contributions.
    *   **Incremental Contribution:** While novel aspects exist, it can be argued that the paper is an incremental improvement over existing diffusion-based SR methods, rather than a radical departure.
    *   **Hyperparameter sensitivity:** As with many GAN and distillation-based methods, the performance of CiDA may be sensitive to the choice of hyperparameters (loss weights, LoRA ranks, etc.), potentially limiting its reproducibility and generalizability.

* **Potential Influence:**

    *   The paper has the potential to influence future research in diffusion-based SR by highlighting the importance of task-specific architectures and training strategies.
    *   The one-step inference and efficiency may make it an attractive option for practical applications where speed is critical.
    *   Other researchers may find the CiD and CiDA losses useful for other generative tasks where consistency and stability are important.

**Justification for Score:**

The paper presents a valuable, albeit incremental, contribution to the field of super-resolution.  The novel combination of established techniques (VAE training, Score Distillation, Adversarial Training, REPA) and the explicit attention to the requirements of SR compared to generic T2I makes the work worthwhile. The experimental results demonstrate impressive performance improvements.  However, the significant reliance on established techniques and potential sensitivity to hyperparameter tuning prevent it from scoring higher. It's unlikely to dramatically alter the course of research, but provides a meaningful advancement.

Score: 7

- **Classification**: cs.CV
- **Score**: 7/10

### AutoMisty: A Multi-Agent LLM Framework for Automated Code Generation in the Misty Social Robot
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2503.06791v1)
- **Authors**: Xiao Wang, Lu Dong, Sahana Rangasrinivasan, Ifeoma Nwogu, Srirangaraj Setlur, Venugopal Govindaraju
- **Abstract**: The social robot's open API allows users to customize open-domain interactions. However, it remains inaccessible to those without programming experience. In this work, we introduce AutoMisty, the first multi-agent collaboration framework powered by large language models (LLMs), to enable the seamless generation of executable Misty robot code from natural language instructions. AutoMisty incorporates four specialized agent modules to manage task decomposition, assignment, problem-solving, and result synthesis. Each agent incorporates a two-layer optimization mechanism, with self-reflection for iterative refinement and human-in-the-loop for better alignment with user preferences. AutoMisty ensures a transparent reasoning process, allowing users to iteratively refine tasks through natural language feedback for precise execution. To evaluate AutoMisty's effectiveness, we designed a benchmark task set spanning four levels of complexity and conducted experiments in a real Misty robot environment. Extensive evaluations demonstrate that AutoMisty not only consistently generates high-quality code but also enables precise code control, significantly outperforming direct reasoning with ChatGPT-4o and ChatGPT-o1. All code, optimized APIs, and experimental videos will be publicly released through the webpage: https://wangxiaoshawn.github.io/AutoMisty.html
- **Summary**: Okay, I will provide a summary and critical evaluation of the paper "AutoMisty: A Multi-Agent LLM Framework for Automated Code Generation in the Misty Social Robot," based on the provided text.

**Summary:**

The paper introduces AutoMisty, a novel multi-agent framework that uses Large Language Models (LLMs) to automate the generation of executable code for the Misty social robot from natural language instructions. AutoMisty comprises four specialized agent modules responsible for task decomposition, assignment, problem-solving, and result synthesis. It uses a two-layer optimization mechanism, incorporating self-reflection and human-in-the-loop feedback to improve code quality and align with user preferences. The framework is evaluated on a custom benchmark of varying complexity levels, demonstrating its ability to generate high-quality code and achieve precise robot control compared to direct reasoning with ChatGPT. The authors also optimized APIs to enable seamless integration with LLMs.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*Novelty:* The paper presents a system that integrates a multi-agent architecture with LLMs for robot code generation. This approach is a reasonable, but incremental, step in the field. Multi-agent LLM systems are actively researched, and applying them to robotics, while practically useful, doesn't necessarily constitute a fundamental breakthrough. The two-layer optimization mechanism (self-reflection and human-in-the-loop) is also not entirely novel; similar techniques are explored in other LLM-based systems and reinforcement learning. Optimizing APIs for LLMs is a beneficial engineering contribution but doesn't represent a substantial conceptual advance.

*Significance:* The practical application of LLMs to robotics is certainly significant. Enabling non-programmers to customize robot behavior via natural language is a valuable goal that could democratize access to robotics technology. The comprehensive evaluation on a custom benchmark with multiple metrics (task completion, user interaction, code efficiency, and user satisfaction) strengthens the work. The fact that AutoMisty achieves a 100% task success rate, even though only on 28 tasks, indicates a significant improvement over existing solutions (ChatGPT). Also, the attention to API optimization will benefit other researchers using similar platforms. The claims that the system operates solely through in-context learning is a benefit in terms of transferability.

*Strengths:*
*   Clear problem statement and well-defined goals.
*   Comprehensive experimental evaluation with appropriate metrics.
*   Practical focus on enabling non-programmers to use social robots.
*   API optimization for LLMs to address shortcomings of prior APIs.
*   Public release of code, APIs, and experimental videos.

*Weaknesses:*
*   Incremental novelty. The core ideas (multi-agent systems, self-reflection, human-in-the-loop) are not entirely new in the context of LLMs.
*   Limited Generalization. While the article states strong generalization capabilities, only 28 benchmark tasks were used. The tasks should be more clearly specified.
*   Limited API optimization. Only 136 APIs optimized.
*   The evaluation focused on only one robot and one application domain, which might limit the scope of its impact on the field.
*   The paper does not clearly discuss the limitations of the framework or directions for future research beyond multi-robot systems.

*Potential Influence:* The paper is likely to influence researchers working on human-robot interaction, LLM-based code generation, and multi-agent systems. The detailed evaluation and API optimization may serve as a valuable resource for others.

**Justification for Score:**

Considering the strengths and weaknesses, the novelty and significance of this work are moderate. While the application to a specific problem (Misty robot customization) is beneficial and the experimental results are promising, the core ideas are not particularly revolutionary. The engineering effort in optimizing the APIs and building the multi-agent framework is commendable, but this alone doesn't justify a very high score. The main concern is incremental contribution, not a revolutionary or game-changing one. This leads to a score on the lower end.

**Score: 6**

- **Classification**: cs.RO
- **Score**: 6/10

### RoboDesign1M: A Large-scale Dataset for Robot Design Understanding
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2503.06796v1)
- **Authors**: Tri Le, Toan Nguyen, Quang Tran, Quang Nguyen, Baoru Huang, Hoan Nguyen, Minh Nhat Vu, Tung D. Ta, Anh Nguyen
- **Abstract**: Robot design is a complex and time-consuming process that requires specialized expertise. Gaining a deeper understanding of robot design data can enable various applications, including automated design generation, retrieving example designs from text, and developing AI-powered design assistants. While recent advancements in foundation models present promising approaches to addressing these challenges, progress in this field is hindered by the lack of large-scale design datasets. In this paper, we introduce RoboDesign1M, a large-scale dataset comprising 1 million samples. Our dataset features multimodal data collected from scientific literature, covering various robotics domains. We propose a semi-automated data collection pipeline, enabling efficient and diverse data acquisition. To assess the effectiveness of RoboDesign1M, we conduct extensive experiments across multiple tasks, including design image generation, visual question answering about designs, and design image retrieval. The results demonstrate that our dataset serves as a challenging new benchmark for design understanding tasks and has the potential to advance research in this field. RoboDesign1M will be released to support further developments in AI-driven robotic design automation.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the "RoboDesign1M: A Large-scale Dataset for Robot Design Understanding" paper:

**Summary:**

The paper introduces RoboDesign1M, a large-scale dataset containing approximately 1 million samples for robot design understanding. The dataset comprises multimodal data (images and text) extracted from scientific literature, covering various robotics domains. The authors propose a semi-automated data collection pipeline to acquire and process this data. To evaluate the dataset's utility, they conduct experiments on tasks like design image generation, visual question answering, and image retrieval. The results suggest that RoboDesign1M is a valuable resource for advancing research in AI-driven robotic design automation. They also provide Visual Instruction-Following Data to boost performance of Multimodal Learning Frameworks.

**Critical Evaluation:**

* **Novelty:** The primary novelty lies in the creation of a *large-scale, robot design-specific dataset* sourced from scientific literature. While there exist datasets for general engineering design, mechanical components, or CAD models, this dataset aims to bridge the gap in interdisciplinary robotic domains with textual descriptions and figures. The collection and processing methodology, including semi-automated filtering and LLM-based instruction-following data generation, is also noteworthy, though not entirely groundbreaking.  The use of OpenAlex and IEEE Xplore is smart and efficient to build such a large dataset. This dataset makes a critical push for the field of robot design automation.
* **Significance:**  The lack of large-scale, domain-specific datasets has been a bottleneck in applying foundation models to robotic design. RoboDesign1M addresses this limitation and has the potential to enable various applications, including automated design generation, intelligent design assistants, and improved information retrieval for design knowledge.  The experiments showcase the dataset's utility across multiple tasks, demonstrating that foundation models fine-tuned on RoboDesign1M can outperform those trained on other datasets in cross-dataset generalization. This suggests the dataset captures relevant features for robot design understanding. The visual question answering tasks are useful for future research direction.
* **Strengths:**
    * **Scale:** The dataset's size (1 million samples) is a significant advantage for training deep learning models.
    * **Multimodality:** The inclusion of both images and text facilitates multi-modal learning approaches, enabling a more comprehensive understanding of robot designs.
    * **Domain-Specificity:** Focusing on robot design allows for the development of specialized models that can capture the nuances of this domain.
    * **Comprehensive Evaluation:** The authors perform extensive experiments across multiple tasks, providing a thorough evaluation of the dataset's utility.
    * **Visual Instruction-Following Data:** Creating this data will encourage other research directions
* **Weaknesses:**
    * **Data Source Limitations:** Relying solely on scientific literature might introduce bias towards academic designs and exclude practical designs from industry. This also likely introduces a bias of the keywords the authors selected from the IEEE Robotic and Automation Letter.
    * **2D Representations:** The dataset primarily consists of 2D drawings and images, which might limit its usefulness for tasks requiring 3D geometric information (e.g., fabrication).
    * **Limited LLM exploration**: Authors experimented with different LLM for the data generation pipeline, however, they didn't explore the prompt that they used for the LLM.
    * **Performance Limitations:** While RoboDesign1M improves performance compared to other datasets in cross-dataset settings, the absolute performance of finetuned models is still relatively low, suggesting that there is substantial room for further improvement in model architectures and training techniques.

* **Potential Influence:**  RoboDesign1M has the potential to stimulate research in AI-driven robotic design automation by providing a valuable resource for training and evaluating foundation models. It can also serve as a benchmark dataset for comparing different approaches to robot design understanding. It will also promote multi-modal methods in robotics.

**Justification:**

The creation of a large-scale, domain-specific dataset is a significant contribution to the field. It addresses a known bottleneck and unlocks new opportunities for research in AI-driven robotic design. While the dataset has limitations (e.g., 2D representations, potential biases), its overall value outweighs these drawbacks. The comprehensive evaluation demonstrates its utility across multiple tasks, further solidifying its significance. However, compared to other large language models, this work could have more advanced analysis.

### Multimodal AI-driven Biomarker for Early Detection of Cancer Cachexia
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2503.06797v1)
- **Authors**: Sabeen Ahmed, Nathan Parker, Margaret Park, Evan W. Davis, Jennifer B. Permuth, Matthew B. Schabath, Yasin Yilmaz, Ghulam Rasool
- **Abstract**: Cancer cachexia is a multifactorial syndrome characterized by progressive muscle wasting, metabolic dysfunction, and systemic inflammation, leading to reduced quality of life and increased mortality. Despite extensive research, no single definitive biomarker exists, as cachexia-related indicators such as serum biomarkers, skeletal muscle measurements, and metabolic abnormalities often overlap with other conditions. Existing composite indices, including the Cancer Cachexia Index (CXI), Modified CXI (mCXI), and Cachexia Score (CASCO), integrate multiple biomarkers but lack standardized thresholds, limiting their clinical utility. This study proposes a multimodal AI-based biomarker for early cancer cachexia detection, leveraging open-source large language models (LLMs) and foundation models trained on medical data. The approach integrates heterogeneous patient data, including demographics, disease status, lab reports, radiological imaging (CT scans), and clinical notes, using a machine learning framework that can handle missing data. Unlike previous AI-based models trained on curated datasets, this method utilizes routinely collected clinical data, enhancing real-world applicability. Additionally, the model incorporates confidence estimation, allowing the identification of cases requiring expert review for precise clinical interpretation. Preliminary findings demonstrate that integrating multiple data modalities improves cachexia prediction accuracy at the time of cancer diagnosis. The AI-based biomarker dynamically adapts to patient-specific factors such as age, race, ethnicity, weight, cancer type, and stage, avoiding the limitations of fixed-threshold biomarkers. This multimodal AI biomarker provides a scalable and clinically viable solution for early cancer cachexia detection, facilitating personalized interventions and potentially improving treatment outcomes and patient survival.
- **Summary**: Okay, here's a summary of the paper, followed by a critical evaluation of its novelty and significance, along with a justified score.

**Summary:**

The paper proposes a multimodal AI-driven biomarker for the early detection of cancer cachexia. The approach leverages open-source large language models (LLMs) and foundation models trained on medical data to integrate heterogeneous patient data including demographics, disease status, lab reports, radiological imaging (CT scans), and clinical notes. Unlike previous AI-based models that rely on curated datasets, this approach utilizes routinely collected clinical data, enhancing real-world applicability. The model also incorporates confidence estimation to flag cases requiring expert review. Preliminary findings suggest that integrating multiple data modalities improves cachexia prediction accuracy at the time of cancer diagnosis. The AI-based biomarker dynamically adapts to patient-specific factors avoiding the limitations of fixed-threshold biomarkers, potentially leading to personalized interventions and improved treatment outcomes.

**Critical Evaluation:**

**Novelty:**

The paper presents a valuable incremental advancement rather than a radical departure from existing work. While the use of LLMs and foundation models for cachexia prediction is not entirely new, the specific combination of data modalities (clinical notes, lab reports, radiology, and demographic data) and the focus on real-world clinical data are a strength.  The integration of confidence estimation is a positive addition, recognizing the limitations of AI and the need for expert oversight. However, the description of the specific LLMs and their integration with the other modalities lacks depth.  The paper does not provide a detailed description of the model architecture and training process beyond stating that a Multilayer Perceptron (MLP) model was employed with drop out regularization and bayesian search for hyperparameter optimization.

**Significance:**

The problem of early cancer cachexia detection is highly significant due to its impact on patient outcomes. A robust and reliable biomarker could greatly improve patient care. The paper's emphasis on using routinely collected clinical data makes the proposed solution more practical and scalable than approaches reliant on specialized research datasets. The preliminary results demonstrating improved prediction accuracy with multimodal integration are promising, even if the reported absolute gains may not be massive. The adaptive nature of the biomarker is also important, as it attempts to overcome the limitations of fixed-threshold approaches. However, the conclusions are based on a relatively small dataset (N=236 PDAC patients) from a single institution, which significantly limits the generalizability of the findings. Further validation on larger, more diverse datasets is crucial. It would be beneficial to investigate other machine learning methods besides Multilayer Perceptron. For example, tree-based methods such as XGBoost, random forest, or gradient boosting may perform better, especially for tabular data.

**Strengths:**

*   Addresses a significant clinical problem.
*   Focuses on real-world applicability by using routinely collected data.
*   Integrates multiple data modalities for improved prediction accuracy.
*   Incorporates confidence estimation for expert review.
*   Demonstrates preliminary results showing improved performance.

**Weaknesses:**

*   Limited novelty in the use of LLMs and foundation models in this general area.
*   Small dataset size and limited patient diversity, restricting generalizability.
*   Lack of depth on the integration of LLMs with the other modalities.
*   Missing details of model architecture and training process.
*   Limited in the ablation study.

**Potential Impact:**

If validated on larger, more diverse datasets, the proposed biomarker could potentially contribute to improved early detection and management of cancer cachexia. The integration of confidence estimation and the focus on real-world data could facilitate the clinical adoption of this AI-driven solution.

**Justification for Score:**

Considering the paper's strengths and weaknesses, I would assign a score of **6**.

*   The paper addresses a relevant clinical problem and proposes a potentially valuable solution.
*   The use of routine clinical data and the integration of confidence estimation are positive aspects.
*   However, the limited novelty, small dataset size, and lack of generalizability restrict the overall significance. The paper feels more like a proof-of-concept demonstration than a fully developed and validated biomarker.
*   A 6 reflects the paper's promise and practical focus, but also acknowledges the need for further research and validation to realize its full potential.

**Score: 6**

- **Classification**: eess.IV
- **Score**: 6/10

### Privacy Auditing of Large Language Models
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2503.06808v1)
- **Authors**: Ashwinee Panda, Xinyu Tang, Milad Nasr, Christopher A. Choquette-Choo, Prateek Mittal
- **Abstract**: Current techniques for privacy auditing of large language models (LLMs) have limited efficacy -- they rely on basic approaches to generate canaries which leads to weak membership inference attacks that in turn give loose lower bounds on the empirical privacy leakage. We develop canaries that are far more effective than those used in prior work under threat models that cover a range of realistic settings. We demonstrate through extensive experiments on multiple families of fine-tuned LLMs that our approach sets a new standard for detection of privacy leakage. For measuring the memorization rate of non-privately trained LLMs, our designed canaries surpass prior approaches. For example, on the Qwen2.5-0.5B model, our designed canaries achieve $49.6\%$ TPR at $1\%$ FPR, vastly surpassing the prior approach's $4.2\%$ TPR at $1\%$ FPR. Our method can be used to provide a privacy audit of $\varepsilon \approx 1$ for a model trained with theoretical $\varepsilon$ of 4. To the best of our knowledge, this is the first time that a privacy audit of LLM training has achieved nontrivial auditing success in the setting where the attacker cannot train shadow models, insert gradient canaries, or access the model at every iteration.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper addresses the problem of privacy auditing in large language models (LLMs).  Current methods for privacy auditing of LLMs rely on generating canary sequences, but these often result in weak membership inference attacks (MIAs), leading to loose lower bounds on privacy leakage. The authors propose a new approach to generate more effective canaries, focusing on creating easily memorized sequences for LLMs. They explore different canary designs, including those based on unigram and n-gram frequencies, model-based approaches, and the introduction of new tokens. They demonstrate that their designed canaries outperform previous approaches in detecting privacy leakage through extensive experiments on multiple fine-tuned LLM families. Notably, they perform the first privacy audit for LLMs in a black-box setting, achieving a non-trivial lower bound on epsilon.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The core contribution lies in the method for constructing more effective canaries for privacy auditing, especially by leveraging information about token frequencies and the model's own predictions. The use of "new tokens" is a particularly interesting technique, guaranteeing high loss during training. While MIAs themselves are well-established, the *application* of these attacks with *specifically designed canaries* to improve privacy auditing of LLMs does add value.  The black-box audit achieving non-trivial privacy lower bounds is also a significant, practically relevant advance. The approach, however, is relatively straightforward, building on existing techniques rather than introducing fundamentally new methods of membership inference or privacy auditing.  Prior approaches often rely on heuristics when measuring memorization, while the approach taken in this work relies on making MIAs as effective as possible.

*   **Significance:** The paper addresses an important problem: understanding and quantifying privacy risks associated with LLMs.  By developing stronger privacy audits, the authors enable model developers to better assess and mitigate potential privacy leakage. This is particularly relevant as LLMs become increasingly integrated into various applications, raising concerns about data privacy. While the techniques proposed are incremental, they lead to tangible improvements in the effectiveness of privacy audits, which is valuable. By measuring how effectively their method produces easy-to-memorize sequences, they're improving the understanding of the worst-case privacy leakage in LLMs. The results challenge conclusions that existing LLMs do not memorize.

*   **Strengths:**

    *   **Empirical Evaluation:** The paper presents thorough experimental results on multiple LLM families and datasets, demonstrating the effectiveness of their approach.
    *   **Practical Relevance:** The proposed canaries are designed to be easy to implement and use in real-world scenarios, making them a practical tool for privacy auditing.
    *   **Clear Writing and Presentation:** The paper is generally well-written and easy to follow, with clear explanations of the methods and results.
    *   **Addresses Discrepancy:** The paper effectively explains and attempts to bridge the gap between memorization studies and data extraction studies.
    *   **Auditing:** Addresses privacy auditing in a black-box setting by relying on input space canaries.
    *   **Auditing:** Achieves nontrivial results in privacy auditing of a model trained to an upper bound.

*   **Weaknesses:**

    *   **Incremental Advance:**  While the canaries are more effective, the core approach relies on existing MIAs and auditing methods. The underlying techniques are not radically new. The reliance on loss-based attacks (rather than more sophisticated MIAs) might limit the achievable lower bounds on privacy leakage.
    *   **Canary Specificity:** The effectiveness of the canaries is sensitive to the specific architecture, training data, and fine-tuning objective of the target LLM.  More research is needed to understand how to create canaries that generalize across different models and training settings.
    *   **Limited Scope:** While it's a significant step, the obtained epsilon lower bound (≈ 1 for a model trained with ε = 4) is still a considerable gap. The work primarily focuses on improving canaries; further research is needed on improving the auditing methods themselves.

*   **Potential Impact:**  The paper has the potential to influence how model developers approach privacy auditing of LLMs. By providing more effective canaries, the authors enable more accurate assessments of privacy leakage, which can inform the design of more privacy-preserving models and training techniques.

**Justification for Score:**

I assign a score of **7**. While the paper doesn't introduce groundbreaking new techniques, it makes a significant practical contribution by developing and demonstrating more effective canaries for privacy auditing of LLMs. The empirical results are compelling, and the black-box auditing results are novel and relevant. The work addresses an important problem with clear practical implications. The main limitation is that the approach is somewhat incremental, relying on existing MIAs and canaries, and further work is needed to develop more robust auditing methods and canaries that generalize across different models. However, the paper shows a clear direction for future research.

Score: 7

- **Classification**: cs.CR
- **Score**: 7/10

### Interactive Tumor Progression Modeling via Sketch-Based Image Editing
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2503.06809v1)
- **Authors**: Gexin Huang, Ruinan Jin, Yucheng Tang, Can Zhao, Tatsuya Harada, Xiaoxiao Li, Gu Lin
- **Abstract**: Accurately visualizing and editing tumor progression in medical imaging is crucial for diagnosis, treatment planning, and clinical communication. To address the challenges of subjectivity and limited precision in existing methods, we propose SkEditTumor, a sketch-based diffusion model for controllable tumor progression editing. By leveraging sketches as structural priors, our method enables precise modifications of tumor regions while maintaining structural integrity and visual realism. We evaluate SkEditTumor on four public datasets - BraTS, LiTS, KiTS, and MSD-Pancreas - covering diverse organs and imaging modalities. Experimental results demonstrate that our method outperforms state-of-the-art baselines, achieving superior image fidelity and segmentation accuracy. Our contributions include a novel integration of sketches with diffusion models for medical image editing, fine-grained control over tumor progression visualization, and extensive validation across multiple datasets, setting a new benchmark in the field.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper introduces "SkEdit Tumor," a novel sketch-based image editing framework for controllable tumor progression modeling using diffusion models. It addresses the limitations of existing methods that struggle with subjectivity, limited user control, and inconsistent outputs in medical image editing. The core idea is to leverage sketches as structural priors to guide tumor modification while preserving structural integrity and visual realism.  The method involves: 1) Sketch Refinement Strategy: refining rough sketches using edge information from segmented tumor regions. 2) VAE: Training a Variational Autoencoder to generate latent space representation.  3) Sketch-Guided Latent Diffusion Model:  Conditionally training diffusion models on sketch information to perform the image editing. The authors validate their approach across multiple datasets (BraTS, LITS, KITS, MSD-Pancreas) and show superior performance compared to state-of-the-art baselines in terms of image fidelity and segmentation accuracy.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The integration of sketch-based control with diffusion models specifically for *tumor progression editing* in medical imaging is a significant step. While sketch-guided image editing and diffusion models exist separately, combining them for this particular application is novel.  Furthermore, the specific sketch refinement strategy and the adaptation of diffusion models to the medical domain contribute to the novelty. The architecture, particularly the incorporation of sketch and reference map encoders within the LDM, and the loss function designed to address the limitations of existing models, showcases innovative design choices.

*   **Significance:**  Accurate tumor progression modeling is crucial for diagnosis, treatment planning, and communication in clinical settings. The "SkEdit Tumor" framework has the potential to improve these processes by allowing clinicians to interactively edit and visualize potential tumor evolutions. The ability to perform fine-grained control over tumor dynamics, which might be missed in existing methods, is highly significant.  By demonstrably improving image fidelity and segmentation accuracy across diverse datasets and modalities, the paper also offers an improved tool for researchers. The availability of the code will enhance reproducibility and facilitate further research.

*   **Strengths:**

    *   **Clear Problem Definition:** The paper clearly identifies the limitations of current methods in medical image editing, such as subjectivity and limited user control.
    *   **Technical Soundness:** The proposed method is well-explained with sufficient technical detail. The design choices, such as using diffusion models and incorporating sketch and reference map encoders, are justified.
    *   **Extensive Validation:** The method is evaluated across four different datasets covering diverse organs and imaging modalities. The consistent superior performance across these datasets supports the robustness and generalizability of the proposed approach.
    *   **Comprehensive Comparisons:** The paper compares against relevant baselines (T2I-Adapter and DiffTumor) and provides both qualitative and quantitative results.

*   **Weaknesses:**

    *   **Limited User Study:** While the paper demonstrates improved image fidelity and segmentation accuracy, a formal user study with clinicians would strengthen the claim that the method improves clinical communication and decision-making.
    *   **2D Limitations:** The current framework is limited to 2D images. Extending the approach to 3D is a necessary step for broader clinical applicability.
    *   **Computational Cost:** While diffusion models offer robust performance, they can be computationally expensive. Optimizing the framework for faster processing could improve its usability in clinical settings.
    *   **Hyperparameter Sensitivity:** The performance of diffusion models can be sensitive to hyperparameter settings.  The paper lacks detailed discussion around hyperparameter tuning or sensitivity analysis.
    *   **Lack of ablation studies**: The value of sketch and structure encoders could be explored more with ablations to assess individual contributions.

*   **Potential Impact:**

    *   The "SkEdit Tumor" framework could be integrated into clinical workflows to aid in diagnosis, treatment planning, and communication.
    *   The approach could inspire further research in sketch-based medical image editing and the use of diffusion models in healthcare applications.
    *   The framework could be extended to other medical imaging tasks, such as simulating the effects of treatment or visualizing anatomical variations.
    *   It has the potential to become a benchmark, providing valuable guidance for future research in medical imaging.

*   **Score Justification:**

Given the novelty of combining sketch-based control with diffusion models for tumor progression editing, the rigorous methodology, and the demonstrated performance across multiple datasets, the paper makes a solid contribution to the field. However, the lack of a user study, the 2D limitation, the absence of a sensitivity analysis, and the need for further computational optimization slightly limit the overall impact.

**Score: 7.5**

- **Classification**: eess.IV
- **Score**: 7/10

### eMoE: Task-aware Memory Efficient Mixture-of-Experts-Based (MoE) Model Inference
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2503.06823v1)
- **Authors**: Suraiya Tairin, Shohaib Mahmud, Haiying Shen, Anand Iyer
- **Abstract**: In recent years, Mixture-of-Experts (MoE) has emerged as an effective approach for enhancing the capacity of deep neural network (DNN) with sub-linear computational costs. However, storing all experts on GPUs incurs significant memory overhead, increasing the monetary cost of MoE-based inference. To address this, we propose eMoE, a memory efficient inference system for MoE-based large language models (LLMs) by leveraging our observations from experiment measurements. eMoE reduces memory usage by predicting and loading only the required experts based on recurrent patterns in expert routing. To reduce loading latency while maintaining accuracy, as we found using the same experts for subsequent prompts has minimal impact on perplexity, eMoE invokes the expert predictor every few prompts rather than for each prompt. In addition, it skips predictions for tasks less sensitive to routing accuracy. Finally, it has task-aware scheduling to minimize inference latency by considering Service Level Objectives (SLOs), task-specific output lengths, and expert loading latencies. Experimental results show that compared to existing systems, eMoE reduces memory consumption by up to 80% while maintaining accuracy and reduces inference latency by up to 17%. It also enables processing prompts 40x longer, batches 4.5x larger, and achieves 1.5x higher throughput.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the provided paper:

**Summary:**

The paper introduces eMoE, a memory-efficient inference system for Mixture-of-Experts (MoE) based large language models (LLMs).  eMoE addresses the high memory demands and associated monetary costs of deploying MoE models by leveraging observations about expert usage patterns. It integrates several components: *Expert Prediction*, proactively loading only the most likely required experts based on prior token-to-expert routing distributions; *Periodic Expert Invocation*, invoking the expert predictor less frequently than every prompt to reduce loading overhead; *Task-aware Expert Loading*, skipping predictions for tasks less sensitive to routing accuracy; and *Task-aware Request Scheduling*, scheduling requests to minimize end-to-end inference latency by considering SLOs, output lengths, and expert loading latencies.  Experiments on popular MoE LLMs show that eMoE reduces memory consumption by up to 80%, reduces latency by up to 17%, enables longer prompt processing and larger batch sizes, and increases throughput compared to existing inference systems like vLLM and DeepSpeed-FastGen.

**Critical Evaluation:**

The paper tackles a relevant and significant problem: the high memory requirements of MoE models, which are a barrier to their widespread deployment.  The proposed eMoE system is well-motivated by empirical observations of token-to-expert routing patterns and task-specific characteristics. The integration of expert prediction, periodic invocation, and task-aware loading and scheduling is a cohesive approach.

**Strengths:**

*   **Practical Relevance:** The paper directly addresses a practical problem hindering the adoption of MoE models. Memory limitations are a crucial bottleneck in real-world LLM deployments.
*   **Empirical Foundation:** The design choices are grounded in empirical analysis of expert activation patterns and task characteristics across various datasets and MoE models. This increases confidence in the effectiveness of the approach.
*   **Cohesive System Design:** The integration of multiple techniques (expert prediction, periodic invocation, task-aware loading, and scheduling) into a single system is well-reasoned and mutually reinforcing.
*   **Significant Performance Gains:** The experimental results demonstrate substantial reductions in memory consumption and inference latency, as well as increased throughput and support for longer prompts and larger batches. These gains are practically significant.
*   **Comparison to State-of-the-Art:** The evaluation includes comparisons to strong baseline systems such as vLLM and DeepSpeed-FastGen, which provides a clear understanding of eMoE's advantages.
*   **Ablation and Sensitivity Analysis:** The paper provides a useful discussion of the impact of different parameters and the sensitivity to task classification accuracy. This helps to understand the trade-offs involved and the robustness of the system.
* **Focuses on Efficiency, not just Raw Accuracy:** The paper takes a system-level approach, showing results for prompts that might be significantly longer than can be handled with existing systems. This emphasis on practical applicability is important.

**Weaknesses:**

*   **Incremental Novelty:** While the combination of techniques is valuable, some of the individual components (e.g., expert prediction based on history) have been explored in related areas, such as caching or prefetching in other contexts. The core novelty lies in the application and integration of these techniques specifically for memory-efficient MoE inference, guided by the unique characteristics of expert routing.
*   **Dataset Specificity:** While several datasets are used, it's possible that the expert activation patterns and task sensitivities observed are specific to the chosen datasets and may not generalize perfectly to all possible tasks or domains.
*   **Model-Specific Tuning**: The "every 40 prompts" observation might be tied to the specifics of training for the used models. Testing the adaptation of this interval would further strengthen the result.
*   **Implementation Details:** While the paper describes the implementation approach, some specific implementation details, such as the precise architecture and training procedure for the expert prediction model, could be elaborated further.

**Overall Significance:**

eMoE represents a significant step towards making MoE models more practical for real-world deployment.  The system-level approach, combined with the empirical foundation and substantial performance gains, makes this a valuable contribution. While the individual components may have some overlap with existing techniques, the specific application and integration within the context of MoE inference, along with the experimental results demonstrating its effectiveness, justify its significance.

**Score: 8**

**Rationale:**

A score of 8 reflects the paper's strong practical relevance, sound methodology, and substantial performance improvements. While the individual components aren't entirely novel, the integration and empirical grounding in MoE characteristics, combined with the demonstrated results, make it a significant and useful contribution. The paper's impact will likely be felt in the broader adoption and deployment of MoE-based LLMs. Future work should address the dataset specificity issue through broader testing and model-specific tuning challenges.

- **Classification**: cs.LG
- **Score**: 8/10

### GUIDE-CoT: Goal-driven and User-Informed Dynamic Estimation for Pedestrian Trajectory using Chain-of-Thought
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2503.06832v1)
- **Authors**: Sungsik Kim, Janghyun Baek, Jinkyu Kim, Jaekoo Lee
- **Abstract**: While Large Language Models (LLMs) have recently shown impressive results in reasoning tasks, their application to pedestrian trajectory prediction remains challenging due to two key limitations: insufficient use of visual information and the difficulty of predicting entire trajectories. To address these challenges, we propose Goal-driven and User-Informed Dynamic Estimation for pedestrian trajectory using Chain-of-Thought (GUIDE-CoT). Our approach integrates two innovative modules: (1) a goal-oriented visual prompt, which enhances goal prediction accuracy combining visual prompts with a pretrained visual encoder, and (2) a chain-of-thought (CoT) LLM for trajectory generation, which generates realistic trajectories toward the predicted goal. Moreover, our method introduces controllable trajectory generation, allowing for flexible and user-guided modifications to the predicted paths. Through extensive experiments on the ETH/UCY benchmark datasets, our method achieves state-of-the-art performance, delivering both high accuracy and greater adaptability in pedestrian trajectory prediction. Our code is publicly available at https://github.com/ai-kmu/GUIDE-CoT.
- **Summary**: Here is a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper "GUIDE-CoT: Goal-driven and User-Informed Dynamic Estimation for Pedestrian Trajectory using Chain-of-Thought" proposes a new approach to pedestrian trajectory prediction using Large Language Models (LLMs). The method, named GUIDE-CoT, addresses limitations of existing LLM-based trajectory prediction methods by incorporating a goal-oriented visual prompt and a chain-of-thought (CoT) LLM for trajectory generation.  The goal-oriented visual prompt combines visual cues with a visual encoder to improve the accuracy of goal prediction. The CoT LLM generates realistic trajectories towards the predicted goal.  The method also allows for controllable trajectory generation, enabling users to modify predicted paths. Experiments on the ETH/UCY benchmark datasets demonstrate state-of-the-art performance.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:**  The core novelty of this paper lies in the integration of a goal-oriented visual prompt with a chain-of-thought LLM for pedestrian trajectory prediction. While LLMs have been previously used for this task, the specific combination and the way the visual information is incorporated via visual prompts is new. The controllable trajectory generation through user guidance is also a novel feature.
*   **Significance:** The paper addresses a crucial problem in autonomous driving and urban planning. Accurate pedestrian trajectory prediction is essential for safety and efficiency. Improving upon existing LLM-based methods to better incorporate visual information and predict entire trajectories is a significant contribution. The user-guided control adds practical value. The paper's results demonstrating state-of-the-art performance further solidify its significance.
*   **Strengths:**
    *   Strong empirical results on standard datasets (ETH/UCY).
    *   Addresses key limitations of existing LLM-based trajectory prediction methods.
    *   Introduces a practical feature for controllable trajectory generation.
    *   Clear and well-organized presentation.
*   **Weaknesses:**
    *   The ablation study (Table 2) is good, but could be improved by ablating each component of the model including visual prompt and semantic maps separately for a more complete assessment of each component's individual contribution.
    *   The reliance on LLMs might introduce computational overhead, which is not thoroughly discussed. Real-time performance might be a challenge.
    *   The environmental factors are not well explored in the paper and the effectiveness of the visual prompts might be environment dependent, which is acknowledged by the authors. Further experiments and discussion around this aspect is important.
    *   While the paper mentions ethical concerns with trajectory prediction, a more robust discussion about the potential misuse of controllable trajectory prediction would strengthen the paper.

*   **Potential Influence:**  The paper has the potential to influence future research in pedestrian trajectory prediction, particularly in LLM-based methods.  The integration of visual prompts is a promising direction.  The idea of user-controllable trajectory prediction might open up new avenues for human-robot interaction in autonomous systems.

*Rationale for the score:*

The paper is a significant contribution to the area of trajectory prediction because it addresses key problems with current LLM methods. The novel usage of visual prompt engineering combined with chain-of-thought provides enhanced performance. The experiments are solid and the user-controlled generation is appealing. The main limitations are regarding computation overhead, lack of discussion surrounding a robust ethical framework, and the need for more elaborate ablation studies to provide more insights into the components. Overall, it demonstrates a significant and novel contribution.

**Score: 8**

- **Classification**: cs.CV
- **Score**: 8/10

### MADS: Multi-Attribute Document Supervision for Zero-Shot Image Classification
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2503.06847v1)
- **Authors**: Xiangyan Qu, Jing Yu, Jiamin Zhuang, Gaopeng Gou, Gang Xiong, Qi Wu
- **Abstract**: Zero-shot learning (ZSL) aims to train a model on seen classes and recognize unseen classes by knowledge transfer through shared auxiliary information. Recent studies reveal that documents from encyclopedias provide helpful auxiliary information. However, existing methods align noisy documents, entangled in visual and non-visual descriptions, with image regions, yet solely depend on implicit learning. These models fail to filter non-visual noise reliably and incorrectly align non-visual words to image regions, which is harmful to knowledge transfer. In this work, we propose a novel multi-attribute document supervision framework to remove noises at both document collection and model learning stages. With the help of large language models, we introduce a novel prompt algorithm that automatically removes non-visual descriptions and enriches less-described documents in multiple attribute views. Our proposed model, MADS, extracts multi-view transferable knowledge with information decoupling and semantic interactions for semantic alignment at local and global levels. Besides, we introduce a model-agnostic focus loss to explicitly enhance attention to visually discriminative information during training, also improving existing methods without additional parameters. With comparable computation costs, MADS consistently outperforms the SOTA by 7.2% and 8.2% on average in three benchmarks for document-based ZSL and GZSL settings, respectively. Moreover, we qualitatively offer interpretable predictions from multiple attribute views.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the provided research paper:

**Summary:**

The paper "MADS: Multi-Attribute Document Supervision for Zero-Shot Image Classification" proposes a new framework, MADS, for improving zero-shot learning (ZSL) that utilizes textual documents as auxiliary information.  MADS addresses the issues of noisy and less-described documents, which often hinder the performance of existing document-based ZSL methods.  The key contributions are: (1) a prompt-based algorithm using Large Language Models (LLMs) to automatically remove non-visual descriptions and decouple semantic information into multiple attribute views; (2) a novel MADS network designed to extract transferable knowledge from these multi-attribute documents, achieving semantic alignment at both local and global levels, coupled with a focus loss that explicitly encourages attention to visually discriminative information; (3) consistent outperformance of state-of-the-art methods on multiple ZSL benchmarks, accompanied by interpretable qualitative results.  The method removes non-visual noise and enriches less-described documents with LLMs.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The core novelty lies in the multi-attribute document supervision framework and the specific prompting strategy for LLMs to remove noise and enrich documents. Decomposing the complex task of document cleaning into smaller, more manageable sub-tasks through prompt engineering is a significant improvement. The focus loss is model-agnostic and also a novel strategy.

*   **Significance:**  Document-based ZSL is a promising approach, but often suffers from the issue of noisy documents. MADS tackles this limitation effectively, which enables better knowledge transfer from textual documents to image recognition. The qualitative results add further support and interpretability to the method. The consistent performance gains across multiple datasets, especially with respect to the fine-grained classification tasks of CUB and FLO, are significant. Also noteworthy is that this is achieved with comparable computational costs to existing methods.

*   **Strengths:**
    *   **Effective Noise Reduction:** The multi-attribute document supervision effectively reduces noise at both the document collection and model learning stages.
    *   **Improved Semantic Alignment:** The MADS network effectively aligns visual words with corresponding image regions at global and local levels.
    *   **Model-Agnostic Focus Loss:** The focus loss encourages the model to attend to visually discriminative information, improving performance and interpretability.
    *   **Strong Empirical Results:** The model outperforms the state-of-the-art on multiple ZSL and GZSL benchmarks.
    *   **Qualitative Analysis:** Visualization of interpretable scores, attention regions, and attended words.
    *   **Practical LLM Approach:** the design of the algorithm that interacts with Large Language Models is novel in and of itself.

*   **Weaknesses:**
    *   **Dependence on LLMs:** The framework relies on LLMs, making it susceptible to the limitations and biases inherent in these models.
    *   **Complexity:** While the paper makes an effort to decompose tasks in LLM interaction, the prompt design and the overall pipeline remain intricate. It is not clear how robust the method is to changes in the LLM, or how the prompt must be adapted.
    *   **Generalizability of LLM Prompt to New Domains:** The current method provides specific LLM prompts for animal, bird, and flower datasets. While impressive, the generalizability of this approach remains to be seen.

*   **Justification of Score:** The paper provides a significant contribution to document-based ZSL by addressing the critical issue of noisy documents. The improvements of existing methods with the focus loss demonstrate the effectiveness of the approach. While the method relies heavily on LLMs, the proposed prompt engineering strategy, the MADS network architecture, and the strong empirical results warrant a high score.

Score: 8

- **Classification**: cs.CV
- **Score**: 8/10

### Towards Generalization of Tactile Image Generation: Reference-Free Evaluation in a Leakage-Free Setting
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2503.06860v1)
- **Authors**: Cagri Gungor, Derek Eppinger, Adriana Kovashka
- **Abstract**: Tactile sensing, which relies on direct physical contact, is critical for human perception and underpins applications in computer vision, robotics, and multimodal learning. Because tactile data is often scarce and costly to acquire, generating synthetic tactile images provides a scalable solution to augment real-world measurements. However, ensuring robust generalization in synthesizing tactile images-capturing subtle, material-specific contact features-remains challenging. We demonstrate that overlapping training and test samples in commonly used datasets inflate performance metrics, obscuring the true generalizability of tactile models. To address this, we propose a leakage-free evaluation protocol coupled with novel, reference-free metrics-TMMD, I-TMMD, CI-TMMD, and D-TMMD-tailored for tactile generation. Moreover, we propose a vision-to-touch generation method that leverages text as an intermediate modality by incorporating concise, material-specific descriptions during training to better capture essential tactile features. Experiments on two popular visuo-tactile datasets, Touch and Go and HCT, show that our approach achieves superior performance and enhanced generalization in a leakage-free setting.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**
